# OPS-16 — Texas complete and out the door: plan of record

## Why this document exists

Every prior version of "the plan" lived in prose across five documents, and the program's measured base rate is that prose-shaped controls run 0-for-3 while structure-shaped controls run 1-for-1. This document is the structural answer: a frozen baseline table with stable row IDs, each row naming the gate item it serves and the pass/fail instrument that grades it, plus an append-only amendment log. Drift becomes a diff you can read instead of a feeling.

**Definition of done.** All 13 done-card items (DC-1 through DC-13) in `_decisions/2026-08-11_texas_flush_launch_gate_amendment.md` grade PASS via their named instruments, plus the Layer 5 market rows below. Measured-everywhere, not filled-everywhere. `texasCompletenessPct` is informational only (DC-12). A row graded by narration or doc assertion fails.

## Governing rules

1. **The baseline table never gets edited.** Every scope change (add, move, remove, re-層, re-scope) is a dated row in the AMENDMENTS table. What we believed on 2026-08-12 stays visible forever.
2. **No dispatch without a row.** Every dispatch names `PLAN-ROW: P-xx` in its preamble. Candidate for canon-gate hook enforcement (hook-shaped controls are the only class that has worked here).
3. **Statuses re-grade only by instrument** (ledger GET, SQL, gh, live probe) at session close. A status changed by narration is a defect. The GRADE LOG below records each grading pass.
4. **Lane-planner fan model (operator ruling 2026-08-12).** Hand-carried lane agents are planning agents that may spawn sub-agents, DEVIATING from the standing no-nesting default by explicit operator ruling for this program. Conditions that keep the old lesson alive: the lane planner supervises every sub-agent to completion (a coordinator that fans and returns abandons its workers), verification is never delegated below the lane planner, the lane planner adversarially reviews sub-agent output with at least two checkpoints, and the lane delivers a machine-checkable close artifact at a named `_inbox/` path.
5. **Write-slot law.** One atoms bulk-writer slot per database. Slot handoffs recorded in `_STATE.md`. Acquisition, staging, builds, and dry-runs are slot-free and parallel; only `--apply` against the atoms store queues.

## Write-path tee order (advisory; resequencing is not a scope change)

The atoms slot is the long pole. The goal is that slot occupancy is pure write time: every apply enters the slot with a merged writer on main, dry-run evidence, a ready runner, and a preflight gate.

Current chain: A2 SD (FLIGHT) -> A2 rail-corridor -> RELEASE -> flood metros (P-07) -> flood remainder (P-08) -> footprint (P-09) -> rrc-wells (P-11, gated on P-10 merge) -> rrc-pipelines (P-12) -> mud scorer (gated on P-04 ruling) -> easement/envelope applies as they become ready.

| A-056 | 2026-08-31 | P-87 | **The card shipped with a STARVED read, found by the operator inside an hour, and the fix is the lesson A-055 should have drawn.** Operator connected Claude and the card still showed setup instructions. It was right to: the connection read was a mount-only `useEffect` with `[]` deps, so it rendered a decision computed BEFORE the connect and could never revisit it. Reopening did not help either, because the dock COLLAPSE keeps its content mounted (`maxHeight: isOpen ? 100000 : 0`) rather than unmounting; only a full page reload ever re-read. This is the enforcement doctrine's own category: the mechanism existed, was correct, and never fired again, and it passed every test in the suite while being incapable of running twice. **Fix (hauska-map #318, merged `7772706`, deployed, bundle `index-DZW9ff_o` to `index-BS3cC4j_`):** re-read on `focus` and on `visibilitychange`, since connecting happens in ANOTHER APPLICATION and the return to the tab is the signal; a HIDDEN document deliberately does not trigger a read, because a background tab waking is not a user returning. Plus a manual `Already connected? Check again`, because focus events do not fire in every window arrangement and the user should not depend on us guessing window semantics. | A-070 | 2026-09-01 | P-105 ADDED, P-87, P-88 | **P-105 ADDED: the share becomes a doorway, for a person AND for a model.** Operator 2026-09-01 explored what the receiving-agent prose could be and ruled it a build. **The finding that opened it:** a share is the highest-intent moment the product gets, and it currently ends in a static document whose live-view link is RELATIVE (`/share?g=...`) and therefore unresolvable by a foreign model. **The bridge already exists on the wrong side of the product.** Claude Sync (P-87) turns a parcel into a Claude conversation calling `get_smart_site`, which is one of the three tools in `APP_HOST_TOOLS` that mounts the Smart Site panel via `appMetaFor`. That path ships. It lives on the WORKBENCH, which is the sharer; the RECIPIENT gets nothing. The design was already written 2026-08-30 (`_sessions/2026-08-30_p91_v3_mcp_wave_claude_code.md`: carry the address, the parcel node id, and the share URL as fallback, so a connector user gets the panel and a non-connector user still gets an answer) and the lane was never started. **Verified live 2026-09-01 against a real grant:** `/s/{grantId}` serves three formats off one instrument (HTML 3,783 / JSON 4,770 / Markdown 3,033 bytes; `?format=agent` returns the markdown), a bogus grant 403s BEFORE negotiating which is the correct fail-closed order, and `smartsite.cloud/llms.txt` is live and documents the contract. `/share#token` is human-only by construction, the token being in the URL hash. **The distinction the card turns on:** the human share and the agent share need DIFFERENT handoffs. A person can click, so they get the Sync clipboard-then-open handoff built by REUSING `claudeSync.ts` (a second prompt builder is the defect). A model cannot click, so a `claude://` link is a category error; it needs the connector identity, the parcel node id and the tool name, phrased as DATA PLUS AN OFFER and never as a directive, because this body runs inside somebody else's model. **Six defects found in the live agent body:** Artifacts and Withholdings duplicate four lines verbatim; raw internal errors ship to customers including a literal `(404)` and "Call `refresh_parcel_dossier_export` first" which instructs a foreign model to invoke a tool it cannot reach; one line asserts both that the sharer chose not to share and that the artifact does not exist; absence is one string where there are at least four distinct reasons each implying a different next action; the live-view link is relative; and nothing offers the connector. **Two things the card must MEASURE, not assume:** our own records disagree on web prefill (the 2026-08-30 session reports `https://claude.ai/new?q=` tested and executing; `_decisions/2026-08-31_claude_sync_card_and_connected_signal.md` records it undocumented and reportedly removed Oct 2025, choosing clipboard-first) and the lane reports which is true as observed or reports it unresolved rather than quietly adopting either; and whether the panel mounts when the prefilled call is the first action in a fresh conversation, which is almost certainly yes and is unobserved. Clipboard-first is correct regardless, so this is copy, not architecture. **P-88 note:** this makes every share a doorway, so the directory listing becomes an amplifier rather than the only way in. Card `_inbox/2026-09-01_p105_share_handoff_WDLL.md`. | Operator 2026-09-01 "i want to explore and brainstorm what is possible with it... this is used to drive an amazing smartsite experience inside of claude", then "yes investigate" and "yes". | Y |

| A-071 | 2026-09-01 | P-106 ADDED, P-101 | **P-106 ADDED: constraint search, the query shape nobody else can serve.** Operator 2026-09-01, arriving at it from a positioning conversation about vibe-coded real estate apps: the answer is not more features but serving what we already hold better. **The capability gap in one sentence:** today you can look up A PARCEL and you cannot ask a question ACROSS parcels. `find_parcel` takes an address, a bare street or a radius, which is lookup; the land buyer's actual job is "Bastrop County, 2+ acres, outside the floodplain, not ag-exempt." MLS search filters attributes of a LISTING; this filters what you can DO with the land, which is why no competitor can copy it without per-jurisdiction constraint rails. **It is also what makes Studio real:** screens are the list, this is how you BUILD the list, and Studio currently gives a customer somewhere to put parcels and no way to find them. **THIS IS A PROJECTION, NOT A PIPELINE, and that was measured rather than assumed** (reltuples, full counts time out under the active fill writers): txgio_parcel 16,428,786; cad_property 7,599,146; place_layer_snapshots 6,917,372; landing_parcel_jurisdiction 981,405; tx_fema_nfhl_flood_zone 198,704. The data exists and the SHAPE is lookup-only: place_layer_snapshots indexes `(adapter_key, place_key)` and `(adapter_key, lat_rounded, lng_rounded)` and nothing on `payload_json`, so filtering by attribute today is a full scan of 6.9M JSONB rows per query. The work is projecting baked facets into a filterable shape, which is the same shape the Factory's parcel_record cells are already being written in, so the fills are the enabler rather than a competing effort. **Bounded to the six CTX counties for v1:** landing_parcel_jurisdiction covers 981K of 7.6M parcels, so incorporation is known for roughly 13 percent statewide and a statewide claim is not available. **THE DESIGN IDEA THE CARD EXISTS TO GET RIGHT, and the premium differentiator:** a filter over a rail with gaps must return THREE SETS, never one — matched, excluded, and NOT EVALUATED with the rail named. Including unmeasured parcels fabricates a claim; excluding them silently hides parcels that might qualify while the user believes they saw everything. Both are the defect this operation is named against. "412 match, 89 excluded, 1,203 not evaluated because flood is unmeasured there" is a shape no listing search anywhere produces, and it turns coverage gaps from an embarrassment into a visible honest number that tells a professional exactly where their own diligence is still owed. A response collapsing any two of the three fails the card outright. **Seven acceptance items**, opening with MEASURE BEFORE PROJECTING: the searchable rail set is chosen from a per-rail per-county population table, not from the card's guess, and sentinels count as unmeasured rather than present (the `", ,"` situs is a known live case where a naive non-null test overstates Bastrop coverage by ~21 points). Setbacks, OSSF, utilities, ag rollback and minerals are DELIBERATELY OUT of v1 because they would return almost entirely not-evaluated: honest and useless. The unmeasured-threshold at which a filter is refused is left as an OPERATOR RULING with evidence, not picked silently by the lane. Card `_inbox/2026-09-01_p106_constraint_search_WDLL.md`. **P-101 note:** this inherits whatever rung screens land at and does not carry its own gate. | Operator 2026-09-01: "I don't think it's necessarily adding a whole bunch more stuff, it's more like really refining and fine tuning what we're doing... you're describing unlocking a capability that we already have less as a build, but we have the data, so we need to serve it up better. I like it. Let's do it." | Y |

| A-072 | 2026-09-02 | P-107 ADDED, P-88 | **P-107 ADDED: out-of-coverage is its own miss class, because the masters' coverage safeguard is currently VACUOUS.** Operator ruled option (a) 2026-09-02. **The defect, measured not argued:** `find_parcel` on `1600 E Camelback Rd, Phoenix, AZ` returns `{"hits":[],"missClass":"no-hit"}` and nothing else. The same call on `147 Kahana Ln, Bastrop` — in coverage, no matching parcel — returns `located-unbound` PLUS the address point, so the user learns the address is known and simply does not bind. Out of coverage the product says `no-hit`, which is INDISTINGUISHABLE from "that address does not exist." Absent and unmeasured are being reported identically, which is the collapse the enforcement doctrine exists to prevent, on the product's front door. **Why it is a GTM row and not just a data one:** `_smartsite_masters/06` line 83-85 rules that collateral speaks of coverage as nationwide United States, must NOT enumerate counties, and names the safeguard in its own words — "the product will tell you what it does not know about your parcel, so coverage is a question the product answers, not a question you ask us." That posture is honest ONLY if the safeguard fires. It does not fire outside the six Central Texas counties, so the nationwide claim currently rests on a behaviour the product does not have. **Planner correction recorded:** the P-88 dispatch instructed the lane to write "six counties, never nationwide," which CONTRADICTS master 06. The masters govern claims and win; the dispatch was wrong to override one. The right fix is to make the master's posture true rather than to narrow the claim. **Scope:** `find_parcel` already knows the county and state of the query, so it can distinguish covered-county-no-parcel from outside-coverage. Add the miss class, give it a vocabulary token with display text and agentGuidance the way every other disposition carries one, and make it say which places ARE covered rather than only that this one is not. A user who hears "we do not cover Arizona yet" has been served; a user who hears `no-hit` has been failed. **Separate one-line fix, no ruling needed:** `_smartsite_masters/08` line 53 still says a buyer's jurisdiction "can be confirmed on request," which master 06 RETIRED by name, date and reason on 2026-08-10 because it puts a human in the loop and the humanless ruling forbids it. 08 never got the edit. | Operator 2026-09-02: "Q1 - lets do A - it is the right move." | Y |

| A-073 | 2026-09-02 | P-108 ADDED, P-109 ADDED, P-88, P-104 | **Two gaps found while preparing the connector listing, both real independent of it.** **P-108 ADDED: public pages.** The privacy policy has NO data-retention section on a product about to take live money; Anthropic documents its absence as an immediate directory rejection, but that is the symptom rather than the reason. | A-075 | 2026-09-02 | P-111 ADDED, P-108, MASTERS | **P-111 ADDED: deleting an account ORPHANS the user's saved work, found by reading the schema before writing a privacy policy.** Twelve foreign keys cascade from `users`, but **`pe_saved_properties` and `pe_screens` have NO foreign key at all**, so account deletion leaves them behind — and those are exactly where a customer's notes, chat threads, drawings and exports live. The privacy page now states the limitation plainly, which is the honest interim, but the SCHEMA fix is its own row and it carries data-protection weight, not just tidiness. **Also established by the same read, and it is the reason the page names no retention period:** NOTHING deletes user data on a schedule. Corrected with `gcloud run deploy --revision-suffix=... --no-traffic` (a genuinely new revision, verified by its own fresh boot timestamp) then `services update-traffic ... =100`; production re-probed directly (`/api/property-explorer/v1/billing/checkout` returns `401 authentication_required`, not an infra error) to confirm before telling the operator to retry. **Defect 2 — found by the operator actually clicking through**: the resulting Stripe Custom Checkout page had nowhere to enter a promo code, and the payment-element mount box looked broken (small fixed-height scroll-in-a-scroll with a dashed placeholder border, unfinished-looking, nested inside the modal's own already-scrollable wrapper). The defect is that the 2026-09-03 StratMap reload of `txgio_parcel` replaced every parcel row in all six Central Texas counties and dropped the derived zoning stamp, so `zoning_district` is now SQL NULL on 1,516,110 parcels across Bastrop, Caldwell, Hays, McLennan, Travis and Williamson. The 582,442 baked snapshot rows that still carry a real zoning district are currently the only surviving copy of that derived value, and the conformant bake lane that wrote every one of them has no monotonic guard, so a bake run before a re-stamp would overwrite the last copy with nulls. COMPLETION IS DEFINED SO IT CAN FAIL: for all six counties every bake-input rail is populated or carries a declared honest absence; one Z7 facet bake and one Z8 atom bake have run and been walked; and setbacks, buildable envelopes, building footprints and property boundary edges are verified serving on smartsite.cloud, smartsite-mcp and hauska-mcp-server by field-name reads against live revisions, never by merge state. EIGHT LANES. CTX-A zoning spine (legacy-design-tools): rebase and land the unmerged `feat/zoning-band1` commit, which wires Smithville, Luling, Martindale, Woodcreek, Lakeway and Robinson into ZONING_LAYERS and has no PR and is one ahead and 111 behind, then dry-run and re-stamp all six and verify per county against staged polygon counts. CTX-D geometry and footprint (hauska-engine): the Travis footprint acquisition gap of 73,394 polygons against 500,307 parcels where Williamson holds 233,022 and Hays 209,526; the Travis parcel-node divergence of 304,190 atoms over its CAD roll; no-ring parcels at Travis 119,389, Williamson 91,021, Hays 41,619, Caldwell 23,660 and Bastrop 15,542; the Bastrop GEOMETRY-DIVERGE on 48021:104120. CTX-G ledger truth (hauska-factory): reconcile the `buildingFootprint` rail, which reads 100 percent unaccounted for all six counties including Caldwell where 35,269 real atoms exist, because nothing reconciles the atom estate back to the ledger; repair `county_facet_coverage`, which reports Bastrop zoning at 99.77 percent from a 433-row sample against a real 12.3 and Travis at 0.00 from `sampled=0` with `staleness_flag=false` against a real 46.6, wrong in both directions and the table anyone would scope this bake from; build the `setbackFrontFt` writer, deliberately excluded by factory PR #94 because no job anywhere writes a real value for it, which starves LDT PR #632's `setbacksFact` into a typed refusal for every parcel in every county. CTX-H serving proof (hauska-map, hauska-mcp-server): deploy and verify all three surfaces after the bake. WAVES. Wave 0 is CTX-A's band1 landing plus B, C, D, E, F and G all concurrent. Wave 1 is the stamp, gated on band1. Wave 2 is Z7 staging then walk then production, gated on Wave 1 plus C plus F. Wave 3 is the Z8 atom bake per county, gated on Wave 2 plus B. Wave 4 is deploy and verify, gated on Wave 3 plus E. TWO BAKES, NOT ONE, AND THEY ARE DIFFERENT INSTRUMENTS. Z7 is `nodeFacetBakeTier1Cli` in legacy-design-tools writing `place_layer_snapshots` under `node-facets:tier1`; it consumes NO setback tables, `getSetbackTable` appears in it only in a stale header comment describing deleted code, and its envelope is a permanent decline with `facetCoverage.envelope: false` hardcoded at `nodeFacetTier1Assemble.ts:367` and stripped again at serve by `stripZombieEnvelopeFromFacets`. Z8 is `bake-property-atom-county.mjs` into `emitFromTier1Snapshot` in hauska-engine, which reads Z7's snapshots and resolves setbacks through `getSetbackTableForZoning` against hauska-engine's own `packages/adapters`, now repointed to `@empressaio/setback-corpus`. Z8 is the only bake that produces setbacks and envelopes, and it has no runner in any of the 33 Cloud Run jobs across both projects. A third path, `join-zoning-district-to-parcels.mjs`, does a real point-in-polygon re-derivation from staged polygons and live parcel geometry without needing the stamp column at all, but carries a guard that never overwrites an existing non-absence zoning-fact atom, which today would skip all 981,415 such atoms in the six counties because every one derives from the erased stamp. | A-120 | 2026-09-08 | P-126 ADDED: "merged" is not a terminal state, and nothing measures the gap to "reached a consumer" | **P-126 ADDED. SIX INSTANCES IN ONE WINDOW ACROSS TWO PROGRAMS, AND THE RATE IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN ANY SINGLE CASE.** Framing is doc-repo-31's, verbatim because it is better than the integration seat's list of instances: **"merged" is being treated as a terminal state and it is not one; the terminal state is "reached a consumer", and nothing measures the gap between them.** THE SIX, all of which report as BUILT and all of which are invisible from outside: `buildingFootprint` reconciliation merged as factory PR #99 and never run, with no Cloud Build config and no `parcel_gate_verdict` row anywhere; `setbackFrontFt`'s honest `refused` state ruled and reasoned on 2026-09-05 and never implemented, with no owner; the boundary-edge writer, which could not execute for ANYONE since the v2 lease law landed because `writeBoundaryEdgeAtomsBatch` never accepted a lease parameter at any layer while the function beneath it hard-requires one, so Bastrop's 26,846 atoms necessarily predate it; A-027's setback tables, which landed correctly and reached no customer because nobody scoped the bake that publishes them; and doc-repo-31's three PR #404 fact families (floodplain acreage with a real FIRM panel from NFHL layer 3, SSURGO soil, HIFLD electric territory) merged 2026-09-07, fully tested, with nothing outside each module's own directory importing any of them. Built, tested, invisible. **WHY THIS CLASS IS WORSE THAN ABSENCE, which is the same argument the every-record-starts-with-its-full-shape ruling makes one level down: an ABSENT thing is visible and forces a decision, while a BUILT-AND-UNREACHED thing answers "do we have this" affirmatively, passes review, and enforces or serves nothing. It is the governing rule of ENFORCEMENT.md restated at the module level rather than the control level.** THE INSTRUMENT, and the refinement that makes it an instrument rather than a rule: the check is NOT "does anything import this", it is **"does anything OUTSIDE this module's own directory import it"**. doc-repo-31's three families each had plenty of internal imports and full test coverage, so a naive import check would have passed all three and reported health on exactly the cases it exists to catch. The predicate must exclude the module's own subtree. **THE FALSIFIER IS MANDATORY AND IS PART OF THE ROW, because a check nobody can fail is not a check:** run it against a module KNOWN to be reached and confirm it passes, then against one of the six known instances and confirm it fails. If it cannot distinguish PR #404's families from a genuinely-consumed module, it is not measuring reachability and must not be reported as built -- which would make the instrument its own seventh instance. **THIS LANDS ON AN EXISTING STANDING DECISION RATHER THAN BESIDE ONE.** The operation already ruled that text search cannot answer structural questions and that reachability and ownership need a structural instrument, never a grep. doc-repo-31 arrived at the identical rule independently and from the other end: what made its own instances visible was asking who imports this from outside the module, and it notes that a grep for the module name inside its own directory returns plenty and tells you nothing. Two derivations of one rule from opposite directions is the strongest corroboration available, and it means P-126 is an application of settled doctrine rather than a new policy. **WHAT EXECUTES / TRIGGERS / FAILS**, per the three-question gate: a script over the import graph, run in CI or at lane close, failing non-zero and naming the module when nothing outside its subtree reaches it. Not a review step and not a checklist item; if the answer to "what executes this" is that a human remembers, it is not a control and must not be shipped as one. Bypass, stated rather than discovered: a module reached only through dynamic import, a string-keyed registry, or a Cloud Run job argument will read as unreached, so the instrument needs an explicit declared-entrypoint allowlist and that allowlist is itself a place things can hide. | doc-repo-31 (P-120 reports lanes) generalising a list of four instances the integration seat recorded at its 2026-09-07 close plus two more found in P-120, into the mechanism behind all six, and supplying both the outside-the-subtree refinement and the falsifier requirement. Scope by (jurisdiction, FIELD), NEVER by jurisdiction -- a city is routinely a placeholder on one field and a transcription on the other. **RULING 2: NO 'computed by approximation' cell state.** 3P-14's trigger is the road-frontage acquisition, full stop. Buildable area is the number a customer acts on; a shape-only approximation does not know which edge fronts the street; and a labeled approximation loses its label at the next seam, which already happened to McLennan's StratMap-redistributed dollars. The acquisition now has a named customer and becomes the strongest Phase 3 candidate (3P-16). **P-146 ADDED:** the remediation lane, hauska-factory, re-running S2's own resolver with a per-(jurisdiction,field) classification table, gated never to regress an earned cell. It CARRIES A RETIREMENT ITEM (3P-15): the factory-side table is a workaround for a corpus defect -- storing 999/100 as placeholders is what ENFORCEMENT prohibits -- and retires when the corpus stops storing sentinels and stops flagging transcribed values. | Operator 2026-09-11: "go with your recs for both", against the planner's two recommendations on the corpus audit. | Y |

| A-136 | 2026-09-12 | P-176 ADDED | **COTALITY RE-ENGAGEMENT: measure the vendor before contracting to it.** The operator met Cotality 2026-09-11 and reports a commercial agreement arriving that week: pay per use, no deposit, no upfront dataset purchase, full library via API and MCP, billed monthly in arrears, and explicitly NO renegotiation (reopening terms costs months). Two constraints this repo recorded as binding are narrower than recorded: the Closed-Secure-System clause forbidding outputs reaching end users belongs to the 2026-07-14 MCP **eval** agreement, not the commercial one, and the 100/day ceiling is the eval tier. Probed live 2026-09-12 by the integration seat: all three REST products (`COTALITY_PROPERTY_*`, `COTALITY_SPATIALTILE_*`, `COTALITY_RISKMETER_*`) return `oauth.v2.InvalidClientIdentifier`, confirming the 2026-07-13 extinguish; but **`COTALITY_MCP_UAT_CLIENT_ID`/`_SECRET` in `hauska-prod-497015`, created 2026-07-14T18:53, mint a 200 and have never been probed** — the 07-14 record says creds were never delivered, and they were delivered the same day and left untouched for two months. The live channel is `litellm-mcp-server` v1.0.0 at `mcp-uat.cotality.com`, 11 scope-gated tools across four sub-servers; `pd-get_property_characteristics` returns **84 leaf fields in ONE call** including `landUseCodeDescription` (which OPS-21 D1 verified is underivable from `cad_property` and moved to 3P-13), `standardSubdivisionCode` (the rail whose parser got 0 of 6 in the CTX verdict), and roof/foundation/construction type (no producer anywhere in the bake). The `pr-` trend tools are **geography-keyed, not parcel-keyed** (up to 25 geographies per call), so statewide trend coverage is a fixed monthly cost, not a per-parcel COGS line. The live channel does NOT carry the money rails (sales history, liens, mortgage, per-property AVM, comps, permits, HOA, flood depth) — those were REST products and those keys are dead — so the rails that close the capability gap remain unverified against a working endpoint. Analysis, the 65-rail BUY/MAKE/HAVE division, and the per-call COGS model are in `_research/2026-09-12_cotality_reengagement_division_cogs_and_probe.md`; the rights model generalizing Cotality and ICC into one pipeline is `80_adrs/adr_032_third_party_source_rights_envelope.md` (**proposed, not accepted — nothing builds from it**). **This amendment opens exactly ONE row, and it is a measurement.** It does not contract, does not authorize a vendor fetch path in product code, does not open rails v3, and does not supersede `_decisions/2026-07-13_cotality_swap_public_record_migration.md` — the standing directive that a live-code Cotality failure is a re-route and never a credential rotation stays in force unchanged. | operator 2026-09-11/12; live credential probe and `origin/main` reads of `plan-review` `1af5ac5` and `hauska-mcp-server` `3b5626b` by the integration seat 2026-09-12 | Y |

(4) **The defect list was stale and reading code proved it:** of ten known bake assumptions, FOUR are already fixed and fail closed at `legacy-design-tools` `origin/main` — `shapefile-discover.ts` (all `.shp` discovered, N>1 REFUSES without `--multi-shp=concat`, the Harris fix), `assertTexasWgs84Bbox` (`parse.ts:185`, envelope AND per-feature cell-ceiling), `isNullPlaceholderFeature` (`parse.ts:455`, decline only on the CONJUNCTION of impossible coordinates and no identity), `assertDeclineCeiling` (`parse.ts:327`, fires mid-stream, "should die on the eleventh, not after loading a garbage county"). | P-209 | 2026-09-14 | ADDED | **`salesHistory` IS DECLARED UNAVAILABLE IN TEXAS, AND THE PRODUCT MUST SAY SO.** OPERATOR RULING 2026-09-14: mark it Unavailable, and keep the rail because it will be needed when the product leaves Texas. legacy-design-tools plus the atom contract's refusal vocabulary. Basis, verified at source by the integration seat rather than accepted as a citation: Texas is a non-disclosure state. `Tex. Gov't Code 552.149` excepts real-property sales prices received from a private entity by the comptroller or a chief appraiser from public-information disclosure; `Tex. Tax Code 22.27` makes owner-provided appraisal information confidential including a sale price voluntarily disclosed under a promise of confidentiality. The wider cause is that Texas imposes no mandatory disclosure at all, so in most transactions nothing is filed to begin with. The deed record carries the transfer EVENT; no public record in Texas carries the PRICE. **This is NOT a capability gap and must never re-enter the P-203 roadmap**; the capability roster is SEVEN. Predicate: a Texas parcel's `salesHistory` returns a DECLARED refusal carrying the jurisdiction and both statutory citations, rendered to the customer as "Texas does not publish sale prices" alongside whatever transfer event we do hold, and NEVER as a blank, a null, a zero or a bare absence. **The refusal must be jurisdiction-scoped, not global**: the rail stays live and the same parcel shape in a disclosure state must be able to carry a real value without a code change, because national expansion is the stated reason to keep it. Proven by violation both directions: a Texas parcel refuses with the citation; a non-Texas fixture does not inherit the refusal. Out of scope and explicitly NOT authorised by this row: licensing MLS or any commercial price source, which is a separate commercial decision for the operator with its own terms. | Y |

| A-164 | 2026-09-15 | OPERATOR RULING ON P-233's PUD QUESTION; AUSTIN RE-MEASURED AND THE INTEGRATION SEAT'S OWN FRAMING CORRECTED; P-236 CARDED | **OPERATOR RULING, 2026-09-15: a PUD parcel gets an honest "your setbacks come from your PUD ordinance" message for now, not per-parcel acquisition.** That disposes of the largest of P-233's three buckets (35 districts, 9,485 parcels) without acquiring anything: those parcels stop returning the same silent `404 no-district` that a genuinely unacquired district returns, and start saying the specific true thing. Measured live 2026-09-15, anonymously, no token: Austin's public org `0L95CJ0VTaxqcmED` serves `PLANNINGCADASTRE_zoning_large_map_scale/FeatureServer/0` at HTTP 200 with **22,504 zoning polygons**, `spatialReference wkid 102739 / latestWkid 2277` (state plane feet, as the thread predicted), and classification fields `ZONING_BASE` and `ZONING_ZTYPE`. **The schema check is the part that matters and it has two halves.** `ZONING_BASE` carries COLLAPSED codes — 32 of them, `SF` and `MF` with no numeric suffix — and our `austin-tx` setback table keys on `SF-1/SF-2/SF-3` and `MF-1..MF-6`, which carry DIFFERENT setbacks; stamping from `ZONING_BASE` would therefore be a fabrication, not a coverage win. **`ZONING_ZTYPE` carries the full code and SF-1, SF-2, SF-3, MF-1, MF-3 and MF-6 are all present**, but across **557 distinct compound values** that append overlay and combining districts (`SF-3-HD-NP`, `MF-4-H-CO`, `CS-1-MU-V-NCCD-ETOD-DBETOD-NP`), so the base code must be PARSED off a prefix. **A naive split on `-` is itself a fabrication risk: it yields `CS` where the base is `CS-1`.** Also observed: `SF-6` exists with 153 polygons and is not in our table, so it is a genuine uncodified district rather than a parser miss. **Conclusion: Austin is recoverable WITHOUT a credential, by an engineering change — a different layer, a different field, and a careful base-code parser — and that path should be tested before any City of Austin share request is escalated.** Path 1 (mint a free ArcGIS Online token) remains viable if needed: the org's `tokenServicesUrl` is `https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/generateToken`, i.e. ArcGIS Online rather than a city portal, so a self-minted token is at least theoretically accepted. **Lockhart is NOT resolved by this and no equivalent public replacement has been searched for — that remains owed, and it must not be quietly folded into Austin's answer.** | Y |

| A-168 | 2026-09-15 | OPERATOR RULING ON P-237: OPTION (a), ACCEPT THE METER TICK — WITH A TRIGGER THAT MUST BE ENFORCED, NOT REMEMBERED | **RULED (operator, 2026-09-15): accept that a genuinely-hollow X-ray refresh consumes a meter tick. X-ray working for the first time outweighs a null row on a rare case. **THE TRIGGER, and it is the part that matters: option (b) — moving metering after the engine's verdict — must land BEFORE any real `perReferenceRateMinor` is set. P-237 is therefore RULED and DEFERRED WITH A NAMED TRIGGER, not closed. the integration seat did NOT verify the rendered polygon on the map itself, which needs a session JWT or a browser, so that verification is OWED and is not claimed here. **P-217 INSTANCE 8, new and measured here:** the SAME tool contradicts ITSELF across depths on the SAME parcel. `get_smart_site` depth **stub** reports `envelope: "present"` for `48453:289990`, while depth **node** on that same parcel refuses the polygon. Stub is the triage read an agent runs across a whole screen, so a screen of parcels reads as having envelopes that the detail read then declines. The likely mechanism is that stub reports the BRIEF SECTION disposition (`setbacks-envelope`, genuinely present at 25/5/10/15 with a matched `setbackRulesFact` citing Austin LDC) while node's overlay reports the POLYGON state, two different things served under one name; a second mechanism, a real inconsistency in one value, is not excluded without reading both producers. Either way a consumer cannot tell them apart, which is the P-217 predicate. |

| P-219 | 2026-09-15 | ADDED | **P1: THE FEASIBILITY AND SITEPLAN ENGINES BUILD EVERY DRAWN LINE ON A REPEALED ORDINANCE.** hauska-engine (report/export pipeline). The facet and the engine disagree about the governing code and **the engine is wrong**. Facet `setbackRulesFact`: 30/10/30/20 with `side_corner_ft`, districtCode SF-1, jurisdictionKey `bastrop-development-code`, citation **Ordinance 2026-06 effective 2026-04-14**, whose own note records that it repealed the B3 Place Types. Engine (feasibility sheets 1, 4, 5, 9; siteplan sheets 1, 2): **25/5/25, no corner-side value at all**, source string `bastrop-per-parcel/34049/front`, citation **Ordinance 2019-51**. Every drawn setback line, the envelope polygon and the buildable-area figure on both PDFs derive from the 2019 values, and the cover of the feasibility study — the first thing a buyer reads — rests on a code repealed five months ago. **SECOND HALF, and it is why a corner lot is the worst case: the engine has no corner-side concept.** Edge 1 is `side_corner` in the facet carrying 20 ft; the site plan labels that line "SIDE 5' (TYP.)". **THIS IS THE ROOT OF FOUR CARDED SYMPTOMS, not a fifth bug:** F24 (the city's own layers disagreeing), P-154 (the conflict row), P-214 (the raw R32 string, whose "expected 5ft" IS this repealed value), and D2's wrong cover figure. Predicate: the engine resolves setbacks from the same `setbackRulesFact` the facet uses, honours `side_corner`, and the per-parcel `bastrop-per-parcel/*` path is RETIRED with a decline and a CI check that fails if it reappears — retirement proven by decline, never by documentation, and the retirement item lands in this row rather than being split. Proven by violation on 48021:34049, a corner lot. **CARRIES D2's PDF HALF, which P-216 did not cover:** P-216 closed against `hauska-map`'s `adaptAtomChainToBakedFacets()` and fixed the FACETS surface; the PDFs still print "19,052 sq ft of buildable area, 64% of the 29,989 sq ft lot" in the largest type on the cover, where `draw.derivedFigures.denies` lists `buildable_area` and `lot_coverage_pct` outright. The figure is barred by the policy the wire enforces AND numerically wrong because it comes off these 2019 setbacks. Decide the policy ONCE: if it prints, it prints off the ruled table and all three surfaces agree; if it is withheld, it comes off both covers. What must not stand is the panel withholding, the facets endpoint having served a zero, and the PDF shouting a number. | Y |

| P-223 | 2026-09-15 | ADDED | **SEV-1 SECURITY: the purchased-records tier gate does not fire, and a bad id leaks the database schema.** legacy-design-tools (`artifacts/smartsite-mcp` plus the records serve layer). (D13) `list_purchased_records` returned a clean `ok` with `jobs: []` instead of the documented `refused` / `upgrade_required`, on what should be a sub-Studio account. The lane wrote "what should be a sub-Studio account" as an assumption and it was wrong. Interrogated further rather than stopping at the convenient answer: `records_request_jobs` holds 42 rows of which the operator owns 2, and BOTH are `status=needs-human`, completed, with **0 artifacts each** — so there were no purchased records to list and the empty result is correct on both readings. (b) **D12's TIER-GATE INFERENCE IS INVALID.** The lane concluded "the tier gate never ran before the query did" from the query being reached. On a Studio account the gate PASSES and reaching the query is correct behaviour, so that observation is not evidence about the gate at all. **What survives is narrower and still real:** a bad `artifactId` produces a RAW UNHANDLED POSTGRES ERROR instead of the documented `refused`/`artifact_not_found` envelope, disclosing `records_request_artifacts` table and column names. (c) **EXPOSURE IS BOUNDED:** `POST https://mcp.smartsite.cloud/mcp` without credentials returns **401**, so this is an authenticated-caller disclosure, not a public one. **WHAT THIS ROW IS NOW:** an error-handling and information-disclosure defect on the records tools, not a paid-tier bypass. The tier gate is UNTESTED in the failing direction and remains so — testing it needs a genuinely sub-Studio account, which is the one thing this measurement could not supply. **Residual product question, not a defect:** should a `needs-human` job with zero artifacts be visible to the user who requested it, rather than rendering as an empty list. Original first-action text retained for the record: confirm the test account's ACTUAL tier. If it is genuinely Studio-or-above, D13 is correct behaviour and only D12 stands; if it is sub-Studio, both stand and the gate is absent. Do not fix before that is known — the whole row's severity turns on it. Predicate: (1) no serve path returns a raw driver error to a caller, proven by violation with a malformed id on every records tool; (2) the tier gate runs BEFORE any store read, proven by violation from a sub-Studio account that never reaches the query; (3) the documented envelope is what a caller actually receives. Note the ordering principle this violates: a gate that runs after the query has already answered the question it was meant to prevent. | Y |

That is OUTCOME A, so step 4's premise is falsified **by measurement** and not by argument. | A-212 | 2026-09-17 | OPERATOR RULING: SHIP WITHOUT COTALITY. AND: WILLIAMSON'S PRODUCTION PUBLISH RETIRED THE ENTIRE COUNTY ON THE CUSTOMER SURFACE; RESTORED FROM A POINT-IN-TIME BRANCH | **The ruling (operator, 2026-09-17).** Cotality is about two weeks out. Phase 0 exit no longer waits on it, and no row may sit idle naming Cotality as its blocker. Every Cotality-dependent rail ships as a DECLARED absence: `unaccounted` at rest, labelled at publish, never a fabricated value, never a silent gap, and never relabelled `absent-verified` to clear a gate. P-267 (ag valuation) and P-283 (read the contract) are deferred by declaration to Phase 1 and are struck from the Phase 0 exit criteria; P-322 carries the customer-facing half. We circle back when the credentials land. **The incident.** Williamson's production publish (run `7b2540c8`, job `factory-bastrop-publish-sxv8r`, started 19:24:19Z) completed and retired **all 602,050 served tier-1 rows for 48491** — the 282,569 R-prefixed rows that were already legitimately retired by the CAD roll, AND the **319,480 numeric rows that were LIVE and carried the 2026-09-10 bake**. The county went dark on the customer surface: `get_smart_site 48491:107190` returned `record_retired` with the basis "no row in the published parcel index" at 19:26:04Z. The mechanism is that the county's published parcel index is R-keyed while its served nodes are numeric-keyed, so a set-difference against that one index found every numeric node missing and retired it — the same presence-shaped retirement class as the 2026-09-15 Bastrop 48021 reconcile already recorded in ENFORCEMENT.md, through a different writer. **The second mechanism I considered and rejected:** that the numeric rows were correctly retired because Williamson's roll had genuinely dropped them. Rejected because the point-in-time branch shows those same rows LIVE with full payloads minutes earlier, and because P-306 established that every R-prefixed key is present in the county's parcel index, so the index is a complete R-keyed roll rather than a shrunken one. **Recovery.** A Neon point-in-time branch at 19:22:29Z (`br-late-rain-apffmnp2`) held the numeric rows LIVE at 2,226-byte payloads against production's 1,579-byte retirement stubs, so facts had been destroyed and a key-level un-retirement would not have restored them; the rows were copied back over `dblink` in ten batches under a heavy-scan lease keyed on the production host, with before and after censuses. The R-prefixed retirements were NOT touched. **What this cost and what it proves.** ENFORCEMENT.md has carried "a mass state change refuses before it lands" since the Bastrop instance on 2026-09-15, and the refusal was never built. Two days later the same class took a second county through a different writer. A doctrine paragraph is not a control. P-320 builds the refusal. | Y |

It computes retirement as a set-difference of served `place_key`s against the published parcel index, with no test that the two are in the same keyspace. Williamson serves numeric prop_ids and indexes R-prefixed account numbers, so 319,480 live nodes were absent by construction and every one was retired.
